L562026-09-29Beacon layout: the corrected concourse ring is adopted; it supersedes layout A (L44) 0.12B1 (2.5, 4.5), B2 (6.3, 3.5), B3 (11.5, 4.0), B4 (5.5, 7.5), B5 (9.5, 7.5): L54's ring moved by 0.1–0.8 m by a concourse-only local search. All five are on the concourse and at least 3.9 m apart.
  • Door-level simulation (spike RfModel and grid HMM; base case +4 dBm, usable ≥ −90 dBm, 3 dB per wall). Correlated noise is a 4 dB per-beacon offset held for 5 s plus 4 dB per reading: a DESIGN CHOICE, UNVERIFIED.
    • A (L44) / ring as proposed / corrected ring:
    • concourse heard by ≥ 3: 99 / 98 / 99%
    • concourse HDOP p95: 1.73 / 1.60 / 1.52
    • worst door-to-beacon distance: 2.9 / 1.9 / 2.0 m
    • right door with noise-free readings: 6/7 / 5/7 / 7/7
    • right door under correlated noise: 74 / 71 / 78%
  • Why not the ring as proposed: noise-free readings at the D2 and D8 door marks resolved to D3.
  • Alternatives:
    • "Hall-anchored": 82% right door, but 74% three-beacon coverage at 0 dBm or 6 dB per wall.
    • "Stores allowed": 86% right door, but four beacons inside stores (tenant space) and 18–21% coverage in the harsh cases.
  • Door marks M21–M27 sit about 0.5 m outside D1, D2, D3, D7, D8, D9 and D11 on the concourse side. M1–M20 are kept. M1 is 0.1 m from M21 and can be retired at the product owner's call.
  • Open:
    • The hall doors (D2, D3, D8, 1–2 m apart) are the weakest at 46–68%.
    • The 6th-beacon advice (C6) was computed for layout A and has not been re-run for the ring.
    • The simulation ranks layouts; it does not predict accuracy.

L592026-09-29Spike walkway network for later route finding 0.13A hand-specified network of 28 nodes and 27 edges: the concourse backbone plus a spur to each destination door. Each spur ends at the door midpoint by default. The building has no loop, because the desk cluster meets the floating desk, so the "ring" is a U-shaped backbone.
  • Width rules: minimum clear width 0.9 m. Edges 1.2 m or wider are flagged wheelchair-passable; 1.2 m is BP 344's walkway minimum as recalled, UNVERIFIED. Widths are read from ID-01.
  • Wheelchair reach: every destination except Restroom (west) is reachable by wheelchair. That one is reached only through Clothing's 0.95 m gap and the 0.9 m print pocket.
  • On the map: walkable cells lightly shaded, furniture drawn blocked (with a cross), solid edges for wheelchair width and dashed for narrower. Shown by default, with a switch in the ⋮ menu.
  • Editing: "Edit walkway nodes" drags any node, including spur ends. Moves are saved on the phone and included in Export venue JSON. They warn, not block, when an edge leaves the walkable grid or crosses a wall. Edges are edited in the file.
  • Open:
    • Door opening widths are model estimates (D2 and D3 0.7 m, D7 0.6 m, D8 0.75 m, D9 0.8 m, D15 0.87 m). They are not yet checked against a wheelchair door minimum.
    • There is no route finding in the spike. The network is its input.

L612026-09-29Agent roster and workflow: Opus recommends, the product owner rules, Sonnet implements 0.14Claude Code subagents for the spike now and for the product repo from Phase 1.
  • Workflow: the main session (Opus) analyses and recommends with evidence; the product owner rules, interviewed with AskUserQuestion before any delegation. Subagents cannot call AskUserQuestion ("removed from every subagent, even when listed in the tools field", code.claude.com sub-agents docs), so an agent that hits an ambiguity stops and returns its questions, which the main session puts to the product owner.
  • Spike (created): spike-coder, spike-tester and run-analyst. spike-coder-heavy is deferred until spike-coder fails twice or the trial shows xhigh helps. spike-builder is dropped; the main session runs the debug build.
  • Product (Phase 1): verifier first, read-only, the §8.6 minimum (C3). spec-writer, flutter-coder, api-coder and dod-reviewer come at the first feature turn. dod-reviewer runs REVIEW.md's passes and skips what CI enforces (C4). engine-coder comes after the Phase 0 gate, staged: package, graph and A* first, positioning after D4c and D4d (C5). native-ble comes at first need (L62).
  • Rules every agent carries: scope, evidence, edge cases, ambiguity, done, code quality and separation. They live once in each repo's CLAUDE.md, which subagents load (C9). Rulings:
    • C1: product tests change only when plan.md names the test and the spec change behind it; spike tests are re-baselined only for an approved L-row;
    • C2: extract on the second use, except the parts the standard or Blueprint mandates;
    • C7: penthouse.json's structure is additive, and its values change by L-row;
    • C8: limits are named constants, env values or settings; a product dependency needs a Blueprint pin and, if it's outside §1, an EXCEPTIONS.md entry;
    • Q1: the "plan" that allows Kotlin or positioning changes in the spike is an L-row.

L622026-09-29Agent models: pinned IDs, native-ble on Opus 5.5, coder-model trial 0.14Agents name pinned models: claude-sonnet-5-5 and claude-opus-5-5. The sonnet alias moved to Sonnet 5.5 on its 2026-09-28 release, and aliases follow future releases silently.

Sonnet at xhigh and Sonnet 5.5 against Opus 5.5 are unmeasured. The product owner's measure is token and output efficiency per completed task, not the per-token price.

L642026-09-29Product agents: all seven written now, dormant until Phase 1; amends L61's staging 0.15The product owner asked for the whole product roster now instead of L61's staged roll-out (L61 stays as recorded).
  • Roster: spec-writer (Opus 5.5, high), flutter-coder (Sonnet 5.5, high), api-coder (Sonnet 5.5, high), engine-coder (Sonnet 5.5, xhigh), native-ble (Opus 5.5, high, as L62), test-verifier (Sonnet 5.5, medium) and dod-reviewer (Opus 5.5, high). test-verifier replaces L61's read-only verifier: it writes tests (C1 still governs changes) but never edits production code. Every agent has a tools allowlist; spec-writer has no Bash, and dod-reviewer cannot write.
  • Frontmatter, adapted to the sub-agents docs: effort takes one value, so the conditional efforts become high, and the agent stops and flags a task that needs xhigh. -heavy variants are added only after two failures, as for spike-coder-heavy. The free-text skills and niche lists move into each body's Expertise section, because skills must name installed skills and unknown keys are ignored. The model IDs claude-sonnet-5-5 and claude-opus-5-5 were confirmed on Anthropic's models overview.
  • Shared rules: kept once, in Gabay's CLAUDE.md (C9). They must survive the Phase 1 rewrite.
  • Workflow rulings:
    • Phase 1 starts when the Phase 0 gate is met and the product owner declares it, recorded as Phase 1 declared <date> (L-row) in CLAUDE.md.
    • Acceptance criteria are written as AC-n: <observable outcome>. Verify: <method>.
    • If the spec and plan disagree, the agent stops and asks.
    • The main session writes plan/<id>.md, the product owner approves it, and it cites the spec's Revision: rN. dod-reviewer reviews against that revision and stops if the spec has moved on.
    • engine-coder changes need dod-reviewer and then the product owner's sign-off before merge.
    • A load-bearing RECALLED claim is verified first; the agent asks only if verification fails.
  • Aligned with standing rules:
    • The H2 settings (TX +4 dBm, 100 ms) are the calibration baseline. On drift, the agent stops and asks and never re-calibrates unasked.
    • native-ble writes Kotlin only until the macOS CI exists (C10), scans in the foreground only (Blueprint Foreground rule), and reads the UUID, Major and Minor from venue config.
    • engine-coder's positioning work waits for D4c and D4d (C5).
    • Versions come from the Blueprint Part 1 pins, not from gabay_spike.

L652026-09-29Multi-venue spike: separate places, beacon auto-detect, manual pick, per-venue data 0.16The spike (D7, throwaway) loads one of several venues, detects the current one from the beacons, and offers a manual pick.
  • Venues:
    • Each venue is a separate place, set up and measured on its own and never live at the same time as another. There is no multi-floor simulation.
    • The penthouse is unchanged: spike UUID, Major 1, B1–B5 as Minor 1–5, about 123 m², M1–M27.
    • A new place's beacon count, layout and settings come after its plan and furniture are analysed, in its own venue JSON. Auto-detect needs a shared spike UUID and a unique Major per place; a different UUID needs a Kotlin L-row.
  • Index:
    • assets/venue/index.json holds {id, name, spikeUuid, major, file}. The penthouse (penthouse) is the only entry, with no placeholder.
    • A test fails if the index and a venue file differ on spikeUuid or major.
    • The test-only fixture test/fixtures/fixture_venue.json has 7 beacons at Major 9, so nothing hard-codes 5.
    • underlay becomes optional (an additive change).
  • Detection:
    • A pure VenueDetector scores readings under the spike UUID as readings per Major in the window ÷ that venue's beacon count.
    • The leader needs at least 2.0× the runner-up for a 5 s dwell, with a 5 s window. All three are named constants, recorded in each run.
    • Unknown Majors and MAC-assigned transmitters never feed detection.
  • Modes:
    • Automatic (the default) switches with a brief note.
    • Pinned only suggests, with a Switch / Stay banner. After "Stay", the same venue isn't suggested again until another venue dominates or 2 min pass. "Automatic" un-pins.
    • Startup opens the last venue. With none saved, it opens the only venue, or the picker if there are several.
    • A bad venue file shows an error, and the app stays on the current venue.
  • Switching: the existing _buildPositioning path. Fixes are cleared, and the smoothed user dot is reset.
  • Per-venue data:
    • v:<venueId>: prefixes beaconPos:, walkNode:, store:, assign:, ul.* and underlayAspect. The PNG cache becomes underlay_<venueId>_p<page>_2400.png.
    • These stay global: textMode, scanFiltered and map.*.
    • A one-off, idempotent migration moves the existing data to penthouse (marker migration.venuePrefix.v1).
  • Matching: one shared matchBeacon replaces three copies (engine.beaconIdFor, discover_page._idFor, and the inline copy at engine.dart:255-258). MAC assignments are per venue.
  • Runs (log and continue):
    • meta gains the venue id. New venue and detect line kinds; existing lines are unchanged.
    • The JSON summary gains venueId, venuesVisited, byVenue and detection (time from scan start to first detection, the counts and the constants).
    • The Runs card shows the venue and the time to first detection.
  • Screens: export writes the active venue. Discover shows the active venue and each transmitter's Major, and flags other-venue and unknown beacons.
  • Authorised changes: lib/engine/engine.dart, lib/engine/venue_state.dart and the new lib/engine/venue_detector.dart. lib/position/ and android/ are not changed: the scan filter is the iBeacon prefix plus the UUID (BleSensorChannel.kt:302-306).
  • Tests: existing tests may change their setup only, with the same expected values, and each change is listed.
  • Deferred: the arrival button, false-switch and flip-flop metrics (window 30 s), barometer, QR/NFC, Wi-Fi, GPS and per-place UUIDs. Because of this, the spike cannot settle the hysteresis values or the barometer/QR question.
  • Config:
    • Spike CLAUDE.md rule 7 now reads "scale per venue as its file states".
    • run-analyst.md is unchanged and not approved to change, so the main session reads the detection block itself.

L662026-09-29Spike: route guidance and arrival recording 0.17The spike (D7, throwaway) routes to a destination door over the L59 walkway, detects arrival and logs each arrival for later calibration of the L53 radius and the L55 threshold.
  • Algorithm: A* (Blueprint 4.2; design notes, indoor routing; L37).
    • Cost is edge length: one floor with no connectors makes fastest = shortest. The heuristic is straight-line distance, so A* stays optimal.
    • Runs on the UI isolate over the Walkway lists, not a CSR graph in a worker isolate: about 28 nodes, and isolate timing is Spike 2's job.
    • Edges below minClearWidthM are never used. Pure Dart in lib/route/.
  • Start: the smoothed user dot (userDotProvider), joined to the nearest walkway edge, or a picked mark. A start or destination more than 3 m (kMaxJoinM) from every edge is off the graph.
  • Destination: a destination door (L53). The route ends at that door's spur node.
  • Alternatives: the penalty method (design notes, Deviations §4): ≤ 1.4× the best length, ≤ 60% shared, at most 2, penalty 4×. Found only when the user taps "Other routes" (L37). On the penthouse none exist, because the walkway is a tree (L59).
  • Wheelchair mode: a toggle; wheelchair-flagged edges only. With no such route (D7, Restroom west) the app says so (PRD §6.7).
  • Off-route: 3 consecutive dot updates beyond 3 m recompute the route from the dot. The design notes' 5 m is about half this floor's width.
  • Arrival:
    • Automatic: the dot within 2.0 m of the destination door's midpoint for 2 s (L53), then "You've arrived?" Yes / No, both logged. After a No it re-arms beyond 3 m.
    • Manual: "I've arrived" at any time during a route.
  • Runs (only during a run, as "I'm here"):
    • New route, arrive and arrivalFix line kinds; existing lines are unchanged. arrivalFix gives each method's fix, its error against the door's mark (or midpoint) and the right-door result (L55 logic). These samples stay out of the "I'm here" statistics.
    • The JSON summary gains a routes block: the constants, the counts (started, arrived, auto fired / confirmed / rejected, manual, cancelled, reroutes), the median time to arrive, per-method arrival error and right door, and one record per arrival with its venue id.
    • Without a run, the prompt and the errors still show, and nothing is written.
  • Authorised changes: lib/engine/engine.dart, limited to pass-through logging methods like logBeaconMove, with no positioning change; lib/log/; and .claude/agents/run-analyst.md, which learns the new kinds and block (amends L65's "run-analyst.md is unchanged"). lib/position/, android/, lib/engine/walkway_check.dart and penthouse.json are not changed: the constants are app policy, recorded in each run.
  • Tests: test/fixtures/fixture_venue.json gains a walkway and destination doors (additive); the tests already using it keep their expected values.
  • Closes: L65's deferred arrival button, for routed trips. L55's pass threshold stays open.
Product ownerMatches (every recommended option: dot or mark start, the guide's alternatives rule on request, confirm auto-arrival, wheelchair toggle, log during runs only, 2.0 m for 2 s, 3 m for 3 updates)Interview, 2026-09-29; guide files (Blueprint 4.2, FEATURE_PIPELINE P0-06, design notes, PRD §6.6–§6.7); gabay_spike code check (venue.dart, run_logger.dart, engine.dart, map_page.dart)gabay_spike lib/route/, lib/ui/, lib/log/; .claude/agents/run-analyst.md
L672026-09-30Spike: a calm user dot, logged 0.18Testers saw the blue dot lag, jitter and jump. The fix is in the display layer only: the measured grid HMM and every logged fix are unchanged, so metrics stay comparable with earlier runs.
  • Causes found in the code: the grid HMM's fix flips between room centres when two rooms hold similar probability (currentFix); the display passed every jump through a 1 s EMA; blocked points snapped to 0.5 m cell centres; each 250 ms update restarted a 250 ms ease-out glide.
  • Display filter (lib/ui/):
    • Jump gate: a fix more than 2.5 m from the dot is followed only after 3 consecutive fixes agree within 1.5 m. Until then it only nudges the dot, at most 0.25 m per fix (amended 2026-09-30, below), so one stray fix never moves it noticeably (design notes: "one bad fix never reroutes").
    • Walking vs still: 0.5 s smoothing while walking, 4 s once a step has been seen and none for 2 s (PRD §6.9: "holds still when the shopper stands still"). With no step ever seen there is no step signal, so the dot follows as if walking. Not a hard freeze.
    • Speed cap 1.8 m/s: an accepted jump is crossed at a brisk walk.
    • Edge snap: a blocked point goes to the nearest walkable cell's edge, not its centre.
    • Frame-driven render (0.12 s approach) replaces the restarting glide.
    • All are named constants, recorded in each run.
  • Runs (additive, during a run only): a new dot,t,x,y,state,gated line; a JSON dot block with the constants, per-update movement (median, p95, max), gated fixes, moves over 1 m and the median lag behind the raw HMM fix. Existing lines and keys are unchanged.
  • Authorised changes: lib/engine/engine.dart, one pass-through logDot like logRoute, with no positioning change; lib/log/; .claude/agents/run-analyst.md, which learns the dot line and block. lib/position/, android/ and penthouse.json are not changed.
  • Tests: the DotSmoother and glidePoint tests are replaced because that code is removed (approved in the plan); every other test, including the L66 route flow tests, keeps its expected values.
  • Amended in implementation (2026-09-30): three existing tests (EC4, the station label and the L66 auto-arrival flow) failed against the first build, because they move the dot several metres with no steps. The product owner ruled "do not fix the test, fix the code", so the gate became soft (kDotGatedStepM 0.25 m) and "still" needs step evidence. Those tests pass unchanged.
  • Deferred: room hysteresis in GridHmmMethod.currentFix, its own L-row after this is measured, because it changes the measured method. The pending trips plan (tap-to-set destination, multi-stop) moves to L68.
Product ownerMatches (every recommended option: display layer only, free on the floor with an edge snap, log the dot in runs)Interview, 2026-09-30; gabay_spike code check (grid_hmm.dart, dot_smoother.dart, walkable_snap.dart, user_dot_layer.dart)gabay_spike lib/ui/, lib/log/; .claude/agents/run-analyst.md
L682026-09-30Spike trips: tap-to-set destination, arrived-door start, multi-stop trips 0.20Approved by the product owner for this spike only. It is not a product decision: PRD §6.5 keeps search as the product's way to choose a destination. Each leg is an L66 route, so there is no new routing algorithm.
  • Tap: a map tap proposes a destination door: the tapped room's own destination door, else one door hop through a non-restricted door, else the nearest destination door within 3 m of a concourse tap, else "Tap a store or a door". A confirm follows (Go / Add stop / Cancel). Tapping is off in the edit modes; the long-press store edit is unchanged.
  • Arrived-door start: after a confirmed or manual arrival, that door is the start for the next route (PRD §6.8, "places the shopper at the chosen place's entrance"). The live dot takes over once it is more than 3 m away for 3 consecutive updates (PRD §6.8, "A later confident live position replaces a selected one"). The blue dot and positioning are untouched.
  • Trips: an ordered list of destination doors, in the order tapped, with manual reorder and no optimiser. The next leg waits for Go with a preview, and its timer starts at Go. "No" stays on the leg; off-route reroutes the current leg only. An unreachable stop is warned about when added and skipped at its leg with a note. Wheelchair mode applies to the whole trip. The map shows the current leg solid, later legs faint and numbered stop markers.
  • Constants: kTapDoorRadiusM 3.0, kTapDoorHops 1, kDoorStartReleaseM 3.0, kDoorStartReleaseUpdates 3, recorded in each run's trips.constants. L66's constants are unchanged.
  • Runs (additive, during a run only): new trip and leg lines, joined to the L66 lines by routeId; the route start line's kind gains door, with the door id in the reference column; a JSON trips block; arrival records gain tripId, legNo and startKind. The L66 lines stay byte-identical.

  • Tests: three new test files (tap target, trip list, trip flow) plus additions to the logging tests; no existing test changes.
  • Numbering: drafted on 2026-09-29 as L67 and renumbered L68 when L67 became the calm user dot.
  • Next: the admin side (L70, L71) builds on this; in user roles the tap confirm becomes a store card.

L702026-09-30Spike admin side 1: roles, map editing with drafts and Publish, store status, layout versions 0.21Amends the spike's scope for internal testers on the test phones only. Everything stays on the phone and nothing is ported (L34). The product editor, publishing, roles and analytics remain PRD §5, §6.1–6.3, L41 and L42 (whose legal gate is still pending).
  • Roles: every launch opens a role screen: Admin, then one tile per simulated user (a list constant; three today, User 1–3). Each user has a made-up IP label from the documentation range 192.0.2.x and a random 128-bit ID that keys its data; a reset gives a new ID and keeps the label. The phone's real IP was evaluated and rejected: only the private address is available on the phone, it is shared and changes between networks, the public one needs a server, and it is personal data. Admin has no passcode; this is not security. Settings has "Switch role".
  • User view: a shopper view only: map, destinations, routes, arrival prompts and Settings. Discover, Runs, run start and stop, "I'm here", the method dots and every edit tool (beacons, height, walkway, stores, drawing line-up, export, resets) are Admin only. With no role chosen, as in the tests, the app behaves exactly as before.
  • Editing: replace a store (name, category, colour); add a kiosk drawn inside one concourse room, with its door and an automatic walkway spur (both floors are fully covered by rooms, so a new store can only be a kiosk); remove an added kiosk; move doors; add or remove walkway edges and set widths; node drag.
  • Drafts and versions: layout edits are drafts, validated on each edit and again at Publish; an invalid edit is refused with a message. Only problems the edit adds compared with the bundled file are refused. Publish makes layout version N+1, which the user view uses. The history lists every version; the admin can undo the last draft edit, undo the last publish (republished as a new version) or reset to the bundled file. Versions never repeat. Beacon moves and height stay live and unversioned (L60, L69).
  • Store status: Open, Closed, Under maintenance or Coming soon, plus an optional note. It is live, with no Publish and no version, as PRD §6.14 describes closures ("without republishing the map"). A store that is not Open leaves the destinations and shows a badge. The entrance can't be given a status, because every reachability check starts there.
  • Routes in progress: renamed or recoloured, the route continues; door moved, re-plan; not Open or removed, the route ends with a notice. EndReason gains layoutChanged, statusChanged and roleSwitch. Data is recorded under the version the user was on.

"I'm here" room and door scores keep the file's rooms and doors; arrival right-door scoring in runs uses the published doors.

L712026-10-01Spike admin side 2: activity log, admin dashboard and store analytics 0.22For internal testers on the test phones only, like L70. The product's route analytics stay L42, behind the legal gate, and phone-sourced crowd data stays out of the product (L26).

Files: <docs>/activity/<venueId>/<userId>/<yyyy-mm-dd>.csv and activity/<venueId>/status.csv. Each line carries the clock time, user ID, venue and layout version.

A visit still open when the app closes counts, with its stay dropped, if the user arrived there or had already stayed 10 s. Door-only stores and facilities use a door zone (enter within 1.5 m, leave beyond 3 m), marked "estimated". Kiosks count by their published outline.
    • Status impact: visits and arrivals in equal 1 hour windows before and after each status change, shrunk to the shorter side (and stopped at the next change) when data is short, then marked "partial".
    • Conversion: card opened, route started, arrived, confirmed, per store.

  • Privacy: the tracks are location data about the testers, kept on the phone until deleted. The product's rules (just-in-time, withdrawable consent; pseudonymised traces; the 13-month retention proposal) are not applied in the spike, as ruled.

L742026-10-01Spike manual start position: map pin or "I'm at…" a place 0.24For the spike only. It amends L70's "users start from the dot (or the L68 held door)" and widens L66's start choices. Positioning is unaffected.
  • Setting it:
    • Pin: press and hold the map to drop a "Start here" pin. Inside a store, restroom or staff room, the pin means "I'm at this place", and the start is that room's door nearest the pin. On open floor, the start is the nearest walkway point. Outside the building, the pin is refused with a message.
    • "I'm at…": choose a place from the destination list; the start is its entrance (PRD §6.8, "places the shopper at the chosen place's entrance").
    • When: a "Set my location" button on the map works at any time. With no live dot, Go asks for a start instead of failing with "no position".
  • Display: a separate "You set: here" marker. The displayed dot and positioning are untouched, as with L68's door start.
  • Hand-over: L68's rule. The live dot takes over once it has been more than kDoorStartReleaseM (3 m) away for kDoorStartReleaseUpdates (3) updates (PRD §6.8, "A later confident live position replaces a selected one"). Until then, routes plan from the manual start. With no dot, arrival is manual only.
  • Clearing: on hand-over, on a venue or role switch, when a route ends, and with a "Use my dot" button on the marker.
  • Trips (L68): a start can be set before Go on any leg.
  • Roles: users and Admin. Admin's start picker gains "Pin" and "I'm at…" next to dot, door and mark.
  • Logs (additive): StartKind gains pin and place. The run route start line and L71's activity route line carry the pin's x,y or the place's door id in the reference column. Existing lines stay byte-identical.
  • Authorised changes: lib/route/, lib/log/ (the start kind and its line writing only), lib/ui/, lib/theme.dart (the marker), lib/strings.dart; .claude/agents/run-analyst.md. lib/engine/, lib/position/, android/, penthouse.json, mezz.json and every existing test are not changed.
  • Amended in implementation (rulings 2026-10-01):
    • Hand-over: the live dot takes over only once it has moved more than 3 m from where it was when the start was set, for 3 updates in a row. If there was no dot then, the first dot seen afterwards is the reference. A dot that is merely far from the start no longer clears it. L68's door-start hold is unchanged.
    • Door changes: a layout publish that moves or removes the door a pin or place start uses clears that start, as L68's arrived door does. A pin on open floor is unaffected.
    • Mall-view icons: a long-press on a restroom, staff or entrance icon drops the pin, and a tap shows the name.
    • Admin's start picker: it shows the new rows through an opt-in flag, so the frozen trip_start_sheet tests are unchanged.
    • Start kinds: a pin in a room starts at its nearest door, restricted or not. A room with no door is refused.
    • Tests: 1221 pass, with no existing test edited.
  • Analysis: a pin or place start is given by the shopper, not measured, so run-analyst treats it like a door start, apart from dot starts.
  • Numbering: L73 is held for the demo mode drafted by another session.

L752026-10-01Spike venue editor: create venues to scale, publish, duplicate, import, export 0.25Amends the spike scope for internal testers on the test phones only, like L70; throwaway, nothing ported (L34). The product's map editor stays PRD §5 and §6.1–6.3. It amends L57 ("walls, rooms and doors are not editable") for created venues only.
  • Goal: test anywhere. Admin draws a real place to scale in metres, places beacons and walks it with the live dot. A created venue's published geometry drives positioning. Bundled venues keep L70's rule. Penthouse and MEZZ are reshaped only through a duplicate, and the originals are never changed.
  • Venues tab (Admin):
    • A list with New, Duplicate (any venue, bundled ones included), Import (paste the venue JSON text; no new package), Edit, Export (venue JSON via share; never a drawing) and Delete.
    • Delete applies to created venues only. Their activity files are kept, and the dashboard lists them as "deleted venue".
    • One floor per venue (L65).
  • Editor:
    • Sizing: the floor size is typed in metres first. Drawing is on a metre grid with 0.1 m snapping, a live dimension readout and typed numbers.
    • Shapes: a rectangle tool with corner handles and a point-by-point polygon tool. A shape's corners can be dragged (add a point at an edge midpoint, remove one with a long-press), and the whole shape can be moved, rotated or given typed values.
    • Shared walls: corners at the same spot are linked, so dragging a shared corner moves both rooms. "Detach" unlinks a corner. Moving or rotating a whole shape detaches it. Overlap is refused, and a gap becomes non-walkable floor.
    • Room properties are as in the current model.
    • Doors: placed on a wall or the outline and slid along it, with a typed width (0.9 m by default) and the entrance, restricted and destination flags. A door keeps its fraction along its wall when the wall moves.
    • Walkways: drawn with L70's edge tool and widths. Each door's spur is automatic.
    • Beacons, marks and obstacles: beacons (x, y, Minor), test marks and obstacles (rectangles) can be placed.
    • Signal model: editable per created venue (RSSI@1m, path loss, wall and obstacle loss, sigma, grid cell, beacon height). A new venue starts from the penthouse's values, and a duplicate keeps its source's.
  • Major: the next free Major is proposed and editable. A clash is refused, except that a duplicate may keep its source's Major. The auto-pick then prefers the original, and the copy is chosen by hand.
  • Drafts and Publish:
    • Edits are drafts. Publish makes version N+1, which the map, routing and positioning use. Undo edit, undo publish and history work as in L70.
    • Publish is refused for:
      • broken structure: overlap, a door off its wall, a room outside the floor, an edge through a wall, a door without a spur;
      • unreachable places: no entrance, or an unreachable destination;
      • fewer than 3 beacons.
      No test marks is a warning only.
    • On a created venue, L70's Layout mode is hidden and beacon edits are drafts. Store status stays live.
  • Storage: <docs>/venues/<id>/ holds one JSON file per published version, plus the draft and meta. The ids are c-<hex>. The venue list is the bundled index plus the created venues, and new ones appear without a restart.
  • Runs (additive): meta,t,venueVersion,N at start on a created venue, and a JSON key venue {id, version, created}. Existing lines are byte-identical. A run that spans a publish is confounded.
  • Authorised changes:
    • lib/data/: the index merge, a created-venue store, venue JSON building. Existing keys and the parser are untouched.
    • A new pure lib/venue_editor/.
    • lib/engine/venue_state.dart and engine.dart: the index and source providers become notifiers; the created venue's effective venue.
    • lib/main.dart.
    • lib/log/: the venueVersion line and key.
    • lib/ui/, lib/app.dart, lib/theme.dart, lib/strings.dart; .claude/agents/run-analyst.md.
    lib/position/, android/, penthouse.json, mezz.json, assets/venue/index.json and every existing test are not changed.
  • Amended in implementation (rulings 2026-10-01):
    • Duplicates: for a duplicate, Publish and Import block only problems its source did not already have, so the penthouse's D4 and D8 don't block a copy. A venue built from scratch keeps the full check.
    • Size: the only size limit is 40,000 position-grid squares. Its message suggests a larger grid cell, and there is no fixed side length.
    • Import: an imported venue sharing an existing venue's Major is accepted as that venue's duplicate, and other clashes are refused.
    • Editing created venues: L57's phone-side store edits (long-press, the Stores menu) are hidden for created venues. The Venues tab sits behind a switch that is on in the app, so the existing four-tab test is unchanged.
    • Outside doors: an outside door sits on a room wall that lies on the floor edge.
    • Providers: the index and beacon-count providers stay providers, derived from the bundled values plus a created-venues notifier, because about 20 existing tests override them.
    • Map: on a created venue, the map's beacon button opens the editor's Beacon tool.
  • Limits:
    • Positioning on a venue built on the phone is untested. If lib/position turns out to assume bundled data, the build stops for an amendment.
    • A venue with no walkable room is refused at Publish, because the HMM needs walkable cells.
    • Rotated shapes snap their corners to 0.1 m.
  • Comparability: runs on venues with edited signal-model values are not comparable with the penthouse figures (L58), so run-analyst keeps them apart.
  • Delivery: all at once, as ruled.
Product ownerMatches, with the product owner's changes: all three parts (create, edit later, reshape a copy of a bundled venue); fewer than 3 beacons blocks Publish; the signal model is editable per venue; deletion keeps activity; import by pasting text; delivered all at once. L762026-10-01Spike dot display: glide, walkway pull, cone and halo, route snap, freeze, "Near", safer arrival, turn prompts 0.26Display only, for the spike. It builds on L67, whose filter, jump gate, follower, grid snap and logged fixes are unchanged, and amends L67's "not a hard freeze" and L66's arrival. Positioning (steps along walkways, room hysteresis, the compass and calibration) is L77, drafted for approval after the baseline runs.
  • Stages: the L67 filter, then freeze, walkway pull, route snap, the grid snap (unchanged), a steady-pace glide and the L67 frame follower (unchanged). The glide is layered on top, so the follower and its tests are unchanged.
  • Glide: a steady pace that arrives as the next update lands (about one update of added lag).
  • Walkway pull:
    • Within an edge's half-width, the dot is drawn on the walkway line. Out to 2 m it is pulled part-way.
    • Let-go: beyond 2 m for 3 updates, the dot shows its filtered position.
    • It applies only to corridor edges narrower than 2 m and to door spurs. There is no pull on the concourse or inside rooms.
  • Cone and halo: a cone from the walking track (L67's heading estimator), faded when still; the compass comes with L77. A halo sized from the HMM's halo, smoothed and capped at 6 m.
  • Route snap: while navigating, a dot within 2 m of the route is drawn on it. Let-go: exactly when off-route fires (3 updates beyond 3 m). Routing, off-route and arrival use the unsnapped position.
  • Freeze when still: the dot freezes 2 s after the last step. Let-go: it unfreezes on a step, or when fixes sit more than 3 m away for 5 updates.
  • Uncertain: above a 4 m halo, the label reads "Near <nearest named place>" and the halo widens. At the 6 m cap the dot greys. It returns to normal after the halo has been under 3 m for 3 updates.
  • Arrival: arrive when the HMM room has been the destination room for 2 s. For door-only stores, the radius is 2 m plus the halo, capped at 4 m. Let-go: if another store's door is as close as the destination's, or the halo is over 4 m, the app asks "Are you at X?" instead of auto-arriving. Every existing L66 arrival test must pass unchanged; a conflict stops the build for a ruling.
  • Prompts:
    • About 4 m before a junction where the route turns more than 30°, or a door it passes through, the app prompts with a named landmark ("Turn left at Dunkin").
    • It stays quiet on straights and at junctions the route goes straight through. Prompts are suppressed while the dot is uncertain, with one prompt per point.
    • Delivery: text in the route panel, a short vibration, and speech through flutter_tts. flutter_tts is a new package, approved because Flutter has no built-in text-to-speech. A Settings switch controls speech, off by default.
  • Constants: each is named and written to the run JSON (view.constants). The starting values above are checked against the baseline runs before building, and any change goes to the product owner.
  • Runs (additive):
    • A view,t,x,y,mode,halo line, with mode free, walkway, route, frozen or uncertain.
    • prompt and arrivalAsk lines.
    • A JSON view block with the constants, time per mode and let-go counts, and a separately labelled displayed-dot error at "I'm here" marks.
    • fix, truth and dot lines are byte-identical. Accuracy figures stay on the raw method fixes.
  • Authorised changes: lib/dot/, lib/ui/, lib/route/ (guidance), lib/log/ (additive lines), lib/theme.dart, lib/strings.dart, pubspec.yaml (flutter_tts); .claude/agents/run-analyst.md. lib/position/, lib/engine/, android/, the venue files and every existing test are not changed.
  • Amended in implementation (rulings 2026-10-01):
    • Open areas: a concourse room whose narrower side is over 2.5 m is open, with no walkway pull except on door spurs. This covers the penthouse's Office 1 and the MEZZ Hallway. The penthouse's East strip, Hall and Print station are corridors and pull.
    • Turn threshold: a turn means more than 30°.
    • Door points: there is none at a door between two concourse areas, or at the route's start or end door. A door's landmark is the store it opens into, else the nearest named place.
    • Arrival: a restroom door counts as another store's door for the arrival ask.
    • New provisional values: halo smoothing 1 s, cone fade 0.6 s, glide interval clamp 0.1–1 s.
    • Build: the pure logic was built in parallel with L75, as new files only; wiring follows the L75 commit.
  • Wiring rulings (2026-10-01):
    • Into today's map: L76 is wired into today's flat map now. L78 redoes the drawing layer on its renderer.
    • Speak directions: the switch goes in L78's Me screen, so today's Settings and its tests are unchanged. Until then, speech is off.
    • Turn prompts: a prompt clears 2 m past its decision point along the route, or on a reroute or route end.
    • Authorised: one android/ line, the TTS_SERVICE <queries> intent in AndroidManifest.xml for flutter_tts; and three logging pass-throughs in lib/engine/engine.dart (view, prompt, arrival ask), like L67's dot line.
    • Arrival: the new rule runs beside L66's detector, and either result goes through the let-go ask. The cone is drawn behind L67's arrow.
  • Glide path (ruling 2026-10-05, built under this row, no new L number):
    • Finding: a code read of the dot pipeline found the glide drew a straight chord between targets and the frame snap checked walkable cells only; walls are 0.1 m gaps, thinner than a 0.5 m cell, so the drawn dot cut corners and doorways mid-animation. The freeze (2 s after the last step) does hold a still dot, except when no step has been seen since the last engine reset; room flips are held by L90's hmm-sh.
    • Fix: the glide follows a path over the HMM's walkable grid (breadth-first over its door-aware neighbour lists, then string-pulled; a chord that crosses no wall stays straight), through a pathBetween hook beside the grid-snap hook. The frame driver redraws the glide's point on any frame whose line from the previous drawn point crosses a wall; furniture inside a room is not one. The L67 follower, the view line and every existing test are unchanged. Files: lib/dot/steady_glide.dart, lib/dot/view_pipeline.dart, lib/ui/walkable_path.dart, lib/ui/dot_frame_guard.dart, lib/ui/dot_frame_driver.dart, lib/ui/user_dot_model.dart. Commit: gabay_spike 07c4a52.
    • Open, needs its own row: the 2–3 s hold when walking off from a standstill is mostly engine-side (step-detector latency, the Kotlin 250 ms flush, the RSSI EMA alpha 0.3 in rssi_tracker.dart, and the grid HMM's prior sharpening while still: diffusion 0.01/s, temper 0.35) plus the UI's 500 ms walking tau, one-update glide and 120 ms follower. This row and L77 leave those engine files unchanged. Ruled: run-analyst measures the stages from an existing run before any engine row is drafted.
  • Expectations (RECALLED): BLE indoors gives about 1–3 m median with dense beacons. Google's indoor smoothness comes from sensor fusion and map matching. Smooth motion, corridor following and an honest halo are achievable here; sub-metre truth, a still heading without a compass, and reliable doorway rooms are not.
  • Baseline runs (on build 6aba7b2, before building): the loop three times with marks; standing still at 4 marks; doorways; 2 navigations; a calibration walk at 1 m from each beacon.
  • L77 (drafted, needs approval after the runs): new HMM variants logged beside the raw HMM, which is unchanged: steps along walkways (D4b), room hysteresis, and both combined. Also a rotation-vector compass (android/, lib/sensors) and calibration through an L-row. The dot follows the raw HMM until the product owner rules a switch.
  • Sequencing: built after L75 is committed, because both touch the map screens. L73 is held for demo mode.

L772026-10-01Spike positioning variants: steps along walkways, room hysteresis, gated compass 0.27For the spike. These are new methods, logged beside the grid HMM, which is unchanged together with every logged fix. The dot keeps following hmm (L67) until the product owner rules a switch on run evidence. The constants are provisional and are tuned from the baseline runs.
  • Sensors: android/ streams the rotation-vector sensor and the magnetic-field strength over the existing motion channel, flushed like the accelerometer, and lib/sensors reads them. This is the first compass read in the spike (none before).
  • Compass gate (D4b): a weak hint, used only at walkway junctions. A reading is ignored when the field strength is outside ±10 % of the running session median.
  • hmm-s, steps along walkways (D4b):
    • Each detected step shifts the estimate by the venue's stepLengthM (0.7) along the walkway edge in the direction of travel, taken from the walking track, with the compass at junctions only.
    • In open areas (the concourse, inside rooms), the step follows the walking-track heading. With no heading, it uses today's even spread.
  • hmm-h, room hysteresis (deferred by L67): the room changes only when the new room's probability is at least 1.5× the current room's for 3 updates.
  • hmm-sh: both combined.
  • Runs (additive): the new methods' fix and truth lines under their own method names, a compass line, and the constants in the run JSON. The existing methods' lines are byte-identical.
  • Calibration (L58): fitted by run-analyst from the calibration walk. Any change to penthouse.json values needs its own L-row. More beacons are added only if a spot's median error is over 3 m.
  • Authorised changes:
    • android/: the motion channel's sensor streams.
    • lib/sensors/, and lib/ble/native_bridge.dart (decoding the new motion events only).
    • lib/position/: new methods only; nearest, centroid and hmm are unchanged.
    • lib/engine/: running and logging the new methods, wired after L75 is committed.
    • lib/log/: additive.
    • .claude/agents/run-analyst.md.
    The venue files and every existing test are not changed.
  • Wiring (2026-10-01): the variants run in a separate list beside the engine's three methods, so the method list and existing JSON keys are unchanged; engine.hmm and the dot stay on the raw HMM. The compass line is written once per heading batch, about 5 Hz. One engine update with all six methods takes about 2 ms on the penthouse and MEZZ, and 15–25 ms on a 60 × 40 m created hall, measured on the development machine, not the phone.
  • Order: built in parallel with L75 and L76's logic, in an isolated copy. The engine wiring follows the L75 commit.
  • Limits: the compass indoors can be off by tens of degrees near steel (RECALLED), hence the gate and the junction-only use. Step detection depends on the phone's step detector or the accelerometer fallback.

L782026-10-01Spike 3D navigation: a 2.5D map and a new shell (search, place and route sheets, tabs, Me) 0.28For the spike only. It is for demos, the tester experience and as a product preview, and nothing is ported (L34). It follows gabay_spike mockups/3d_navigation_mockup.html.
  • Map: our own 2.5D renderer, because flutter_map 8.3.2 has no tilt (checked in the package source).
    • The scene comes from the venue's geometry, so created venues (L75) get it too: store, restroom and staff rooms as raised slabs (0.22 m); exterior walls 0.7 m high on unshared room edges, with door gaps; column, shaft and furniture blocks.
    • The dot, route and pins are always drawn on top. Taps go through the exact inverse projection, with pin targets of at least 48 dp.
    • Camera: a fixed angle (20°), pan, zoom, a 3D/flat toggle, follow while navigating, recentre. There is no rotate or heading-up mode.
    • 3D is the default view.
  • Shell:
    • Tabs Places / Map / Test tools / Me. Shoppers see Places / Map / Me. Test tools is a hub: map layers, Beacons, Test walks, Dashboard, Venues, Layout editing (on today's flat map).
    • A search bar over stores, restrooms and named doors.
    • A floor chip that opens the venue picker.
    • A place sheet: Go, Add stop, Cancel, status and note.
    • A route sheet: time to arrive at 1.2 m/s, distance, the next turn prompt, and when expanded the start, alternatives, wheelchair, trip legs, I've arrived and Stop.
    • Me: role and Switch role (the launch role screen and Admin + User 1–3 are kept), wording, a System/Light/Dark setting (System by default), 3D/flat, Speak directions, Delete my data, Show methods.
  • Kept: every existing feature (L66, L68, L70, L74, L76), restyled. Editing (L70 layout, beacons, the L75 editor) stays on the flat map.
  • Colours: a green dotted route and destination pin, with the dot staying terracotta; this amends L72 and L76. Pins are in the terracotta/brown family. The walkway layer is slate blue-grey.
  • Activity (additive): a lookup source search, where picking a search result counts as a card open (this amends L71's "searched"). The typed text is not stored.
  • Switch: the new design is on in the app and off by default, so every existing test runs unchanged. The old screens stay in the code.
  • Performance: a frame-timing readout in Test tools, measured on a profile build. The target is p90 ≤ 16 ms with the dot moving; a miss goes to the product owner before any fallback.
  • Authorised changes: new lib/map3d/ (pure), new lib/ui/map3d/ and lib/ui/shell/, lib/app.dart, lib/main.dart, lib/theme.dart, lib/strings.dart, lib/log/ (the search source, additive), and lib/ui/ where existing state is reused. lib/position/, lib/engine/, android/, lib/data/, the venue files and every existing test are not changed.
  • Amended in implementation (2026-10-02):
    • Drawing order: back walls, slabs and blocks far to near, floor marks, front walls, then the route, pins and dot. The route is drawn after the front walls so walls never hide it, as ruled.
    • Camera: the 20° rotation keeps the mock-up's direction. On the penthouse, one depth overlap cannot be ordered correctly (a shaft in the notch of an L-shaped store): a sliver of one furniture corner shows over the shaft. A test pins it as the only allowed flip; a similar L-shaped created venue may show the same.
    • Taps: in the new design, a pin, slab or floor tap opens the place sheet for every role (logged as source map). A tap within 48 dp of a pin opens that place. The flat map keeps L68's card and dialog.
    • Add stop shows when a trip exists or a route is running, since a running route becomes the first leg (L68).
    • Admin tools on the Map tab: "I'm here", start and stop a test walk, the scan status card and method chips, shared with the flat map. The pinned-mode "Switch venue?" banner shows to every role.
    • Layout editing opens the L75 editor for a created venue and the flat map otherwise.
    • Frame timing is the slower of build and raster per frame, over the last 600 frames, read for Admin from the first frame.
    • Dot: the 2.5D dot shows the cone and no separate arrow, as in the mock-up. Speak directions is in Me only.
    • Colour: the dark staff-room top is #4E312B (mock-up #4A2E28), to meet the contrast test against the floor.
  • Changed after the phone trial (2026-10-02, product owner):
    • Route line: the whole route always shows, with the part already walked faded. Progress along it only moves forward, so a jumping dot can't cut the line. (The build drew only the part ahead of the dot's nearest point, which could cut almost all of it.)
    • Rotation: a free two-finger twist with a compass button that resets it, on the 3D and flat navigation map and on the flat Layout-editing map. This replaces "no rotate". Labels and pins stay upright, and the drawing order is worked out per angle.
  • Expectations: 3D changes how the position looks, not its accuracy. Tilt compresses depth, so errors look smaller towards the far side.
  • Risks: it is the largest build so far. Drawing order can glitch on unusual created venues. Two UIs live in the code while the switch exists. Performance is unknown until measured on the phone.
  • Order: built after the L76 and L77 wiring is committed. L76's drawing layer is redone on the new renderer, and its logic is reused.
  • Plan: the spike's plan file "l78-3d-navigation".

L792026-10-02Routing algorithm: time-weighted A* confirmed for malls over the mock-up's Dijkstra; four engine gaps logged 0.29The product owner asked which router suits a mall: the plan's (L37, L38, Blueprint 4.2) or the one in 3d_navigation_mockup.html.
  • The mock-up's router (graph and shortest):
    • Plain Dijkstra that finds the next node by scanning every unvisited node, so it costs O(V²).
    • Edge costs in straight-line metres, not seconds. Every edge is two-way. One floor.
    • The start snaps to the nearest node by straight line, even through a wall.
    • It computes no alternatives: "No alternative passes the penalty rules" is fixed text.
    This suits the 28-node penthouse, not a mall.
  • Ruling: the product keeps L37/L38 A* over the D6 CSR graph, with the floor-aware heuristic. Evidence is the design notes' benchmark (indoor routing): 5–14× faster than Dijkstra, and optimal on all 3,000 routes. It was not re-run in this pass. The mock-up and the spike are unchanged (L34, L78).
  • RG1, "Nearest X": Blueprint 4.7 names no algorithm. Use Dijkstra with a binary heap, stopping at the first settled node of the target set (for example every CR). It respects one-way edges and the step-free filter. The design notes rate Dijkstra best for "nearest" searches.
  • RG2, heuristic bound:
    • h = floor-plan distance ÷ the fastest horizontal speed of any edge type, plus the cheapest floor-change cost × the number of levels apart.
    • Today walking at 1.2 m/s is the fastest; an escalator is 0.5 m/s along its slope. A moving walkway or a faster speed setting must change the divisor.
    • Penalty multipliers (L66, L80) must be ≥ 1, never a discount.
    • Otherwise A* can return a route that isn't the fastest, which breaks L37. This becomes a shared test vector (One Router).
  • RG3, route origin: snap the origin to the nearest walkway edge that can be reached without crossing a wall (the D6 R*Tree), not to the nearest node by straight line. PRD §6 snaps the dot to walkways, but the route origin was not specified.
  • RG4, walkway clearance (OPEN QUESTION, not ruled):
    • The mock-up drops walkways under 1.2 m in wheelchair mode, as the spike does (L59).
    • PRD §6.7 puts "indoor wheelchair-width guarantees beyond what the admin mapped" out of scope, and step-free mode removes only stairs and escalators.
    • The question: should the L32 publish step compute each edge's clearance, with step-free mode filtering on it? A yes amends PRD §6.7 and P0-07.
  • Expectations: no code changes. RG1–RG3 become engine spec acceptance criteria at Phase 1 (engine-coder).
  • Risks: RG2 is easy to forget when a new edge type or speed setting is added. The test vector guards it.
Product ownerMatches (the plan's router; the gaps logged in plan.html only)Interview, 2026-10-02; code check of 3d_navigation_mockup.html (NODES and EDGES, graph, shortest, the route sheet); L37, L38, L66; design notes (indoor routing, Deviations §4); Blueprint 4.2 and 4.7; PRD §6.7; db/schema.sql NavConnector.IsBidirectionalRouting engine (Blueprint 4.2, 4.7) at Phase 1
L802026-10-02"Another way" on request mid-trip, back to the original route at any time, and a wrong-way notice; the app never offers alternatives on its own 0.29The product owner wants the shopper, not the app, to decide when to look for another route, for example when the way ahead is crowded.
  • Shopper-asked only: alternatives appear only when the shopper taps, before the trip (L37) or during it. This drops the design notes' "after a second deviation" trigger (Deviations §4). The plan governs that document (Blueprint 3.10).
  • "Another way" routes around the rest of the current route:
    1. From the current position, raise the cost of every remaining edge of the current route (the L66 penalty, 4×).
    2. Run A* again.
    3. Keep results that share at most 60% with the current route and take at most 1.4× its time.
    4. Show at most 2, labelled by what differs ("via Elevator B"), and let the shopper pick.
    5. If none pass, say "There's no other way from here" and keep the current route.
    Step-free mode is never broken, because penalties only reorder allowed routes.
  • Original route:
    • While an alternative is active, the route sheet offers "Use original route", so the shopper can change their mind at any time.
    • DESIGN CHOICE: the original is re-run from the current position without the "Another way" penalty, so it is always reachable from where the shopper stands.
    • Alternative: redraw the stored original line and join it at the nearest point. That is simpler, but it can send the shopper backwards once they have moved on.
  • Off route:
    • Auto-refresh stays (PRD §6.6). On a confirmed deviation (L66: 3 consecutive updates beyond the limit, never on 1 bad fix), the app refreshes the directions.
    • It also tells the shopper "You may be going the wrong way" as it shows the new directions.
    • A refresh keeps that trip's "Another way" penalty, so the shopper isn't sent back into what they avoided (design notes, Deviations §2).
  • Wrong way while still on the route:
    • DESIGN CHOICE: if progress along the route falls for the same 3 consecutive updates and by more than a named limit, the app shows the same notice even though the shopper is still on the route.
    • Alternative: a notice on off-route only, which misses someone walking back along the route.
    • The constants are named and tuned in testing.
  • Privacy:
    • The tap and its penalty stay on the phone, for this trip only, and are cleared on arrival or Stop.
    • They are never sent and never become crowd data (PRD §2: "It does not collect crowd data from shoppers' phones"; L26). Crowd-aware routing stays P3, fed by mall sensors.
  • Expectations: shoppers get control in crowds without the app guessing.
  • Risks: a false wrong-way notice on a budget phone with a noisy dot. The 3-update rule and the named limit guard against it.
  • Follow-up (needs a go-ahead): PRD §6.6 acceptance criteria (the mid-trip ask, the original route, the wrong-way notice) and FEATURE_PIPELINE P0-06.
Product ownerMatches the recommended options (route around the rest of the route; keep auto-refresh; its own row), with the product owner's additions: the original route at any time and the wrong-way noticeInterview, 2026-10-02; L26, L37, L66; PRD §2, §6.6, §6.7; design notes (Deviations §1–§4, edge cases)Routing engine and route sheet (Blueprint 4.2) at Phase 1

L862026-10-02Routes are ranked by shortest metres, with tunable connector metre costs; escalator direction follows the real mall 0.34This follows the demo.
  • Ranking (ruled): the default route is the shortest in metres. This reverses L37's fastest-by-travel-time default and supersedes L38: lift waits and escalator speeds no longer steer routes. Their schema columns were removed.
  • Connector costs (ruled): the demo's metre costs, held as settings each mall can tune per connector type: ConnectorType.CostBaseM + CostPerRiseM × the height climbed.
    • DESIGN CHOICE: the default is the demo's height-based formula for its kit venues: lift 30 + 1.5, stairs 34 + 3, escalator 14 + 1, ramp 20 + 2.
    • The alternative is Aurora's flat costs per level (escalator 20, stairs 26, lift 40), which the same columns hold with a rise factor of 0.
    • This is to be confirmed by the product owner.
  • Time to arrive: the route's metre cost at 1.2 m/s, as the demo does.
  • Escalators (ruled): "depending on the real structure of a mall". Each escalator is one-way in its real direction or two-way, as the mall really is (IsBidirectional per connector), and is never used against its direction. The demo treats all edges as two-way.
  • Unchanged:
    • A* stays the engine (L79). Its estimate is now in metres (RG2 restated), and shoppers see no difference.
    • The walkway graph stays generated at publish (L32, ruled), not drawn by hand as in the demo.
  • Expectations: routes match the demo's wherever the venue matches.
  • Risks: a slow lift bank can only be steered by raising its metre cost, because waits are not modelled. Tuning the costs is the mall's lever.

L872026-10-02Other ways, venue alerts and evacuation as in the demo; shopper reports dropped 0.34
  • One other way, unasked (ruled): every route shows one other way, as a dotted amber line and "Shortest / Other way 1". It is found by banning the main route's connectors (or its middle stretch), and is kept only if it is longer by more than 0.5 m and under twice the length. This amends L80's "the app never offers an alternative on its own" and L37's "only when the shopper asks".
  • Find another way (ruled):
    • It avoids the next ~30 m, or the next connector taken as a whole (a setting), instead of L80's rest of the route.
    • It offers up to 3 ways around beside "Your current way", each with +m, +min, level changes and "Goes outside".
    • The overlap rule is kept, so the ways really differ. Ways stay within the demo's 2.2×; L80's 1.4× cap and its "at most 2" are dropped.
    • "Go this way" switches, and Cancel keeps the current way.
    • It also offers similar places, fixed tips and "Get help".
  • Venue alerts: a staff closure or outage on the route ahead is shown and opens the other-ways sheet. Nothing is avoided automatically; the shopper decides.

    • Live status carries staff closures and outages only.
  • Evacuation (ruled): as the demo, so any shopper can start it from the red button. It ships only after the safety-officer and fire-code review (L27 kept).
    • Each mall sets its refuge areas, assembly areas, evacuation-only stairs and whether escalators may be used. The demo's behaviour is the default: escalators allowed, and a stair landing as the refuge.
    • Lifts are never used.
    • Whether the mall can also trigger it for everyone stays open (PRD §6.17).
  • Expectations: shoppers always see a choice, and decide for themselves around problems.
  • Risks:
    • A shopper-triggered evacuation mode could be opened by mistake. The demo's "Stop evacuation" is the only exit, by design.
    • Without shopper reports, blockages reach others only through staff.

  • Risks: clearance is only as good as the drawn geometry and the measured door widths. An unmeasured door (NULL) needs a publish warning.

L892026-10-02Spike venue editor made usable: the floor is the hall, Auto paths, an Entrance tool, auto-fix of small slips, rotation 0.35For the spike only (L34); it amends L75. The product owner could not publish a venue drawn from scratch.
  • Floor is the hall: the whole floor is walkable by default. Stores, restrooms, staff rooms and blocked areas are drawn on it and may overlap it (not each other), as L70's kiosks do. On export a walkable concourse room covering the floor is placed last, so the rooms drawn on it win.
  • Auto paths: one button builds a walkable path network from the entrance to every place door, from the layout grid (read-only, as L70). The paths are ordinary and editable. Running it again asks "Replace all paths?" and rebuilds all of them.
  • Hand paths: each tap joins the last point; taps snap to nearby points, paths and doors.
  • Entrance tool: tap the floor's outer edge or a room's outer wall; it snaps there and becomes the single way in.
  • Auto-fix: rooms snap to the floor edge and to other walls (mid-span too); small gaps and overshoots close; door spurs always end in a walkable cell; a door's rooms are recomputed when rooms move; paths are re-checked after edits. A tap just off a door opens it.
  • Rotation in the editor canvas: twist and a compass reset. Coordinates and snapping stay in floor metres.
  • Existing created venues: the floor becomes walkable from their next publish. Published versions are unchanged.
  • Authorised: the venue editor code (lib/venue_editor/, lib/ui/venues/), lib/layout/ where reused, lib/strings.dart, lib/theme.dart. Not changed: lib/position/, lib/engine/, android/, the bundled venue files and every existing test.
  • Amended in implementation (2026-10-05):
    • Hall: its id is FLOOR (the penthouse and MEZZ already have a HALL). It is added only when the drawn rooms leave floor bare, so a venue whose rooms tile the floor publishes the same file as before.
    • Entrance tool: a tap near the floor edge snaps to it. Where a room's wall lies on the edge, the entrance is that room's door to the street; elsewhere it is a 0.9 m door of the hall. A moved entrance removes the old one if it led to the street, otherwise it becomes a plain door.
    • Paths: a tap on a path splits it; width and delete moved to a long-press. Auto paths replaces every path, including an import's spurs. A door narrower than 0.9 m is reported as unreachable. Auto-fix tolerance is 0.3 m, and typed numbers stay exact.
    • Product owner rulings (2026-10-05):
      • The flat map paints walkway rooms before the others, so stores and L70 kiosks show on top of the hall. This authorises a change in lib/ui/map_page.dart.
      • A door between a store or restroom and the hall is a destination when placed; it can be unticked.
      • A created venue without a hall can be published once to add it; published versions are unchanged.
  • Cause: the editor checked paths on the positioning grid, where only drawn rooms are walkable, touching rooms join only through doors and walls off 0.5 m lines leave blocked strips. About 40% of door spurs landed in a blocked cell. The test fixture avoided all of this by chance.
  • Done when: a from-scratch venue with walls off 0.5 m lines publishes in a test, and on the phone in under 5 minutes.
  • Plan: the spike's plan file "l78-fixes-editor-dot".

L922026-10-02Main reference is now Demo Prototype 4.apk; walking through stores: the mall marks the store, the shopper allows shortcuts, and a store that is the only way in is always used 0.37The product owner: "the updated version now is the Demo Prototype 4.apk".
  • Main reference: Demo Prototype 4.apk replaces version 3 as L85's main reference. Its SHA-1 matches the demo_app release build, so that source is version 4. Everything else in L85–L91 stands. Version 3 is no longer in the Gabay folder.
  • What changed in the demo (source diff, version 3 to 4): one feature, "Shortcuts through stores", and fixes.
    • Version 3 always joined a department store's two doors with a straight line through the shop walls (1.15× its length).
    • Version 4 adds a Me setting, "Shortcuts through stores: Off / Allow", off by default. When allowed, a route goes in by one door, across the store's floor and out by another. The walk across counts 1.25× its length. Only open stores are used, and never for wheelchair routes or evacuations.
    • The step reads "Walk through store (m)" with the store's icon. Find another way goes around the whole store, with the tip "If a store's door is locked or closed, the other ways go around the store." Changing the setting works an active route out again.
    • A new test checks that no walkway in any venue crosses a wall. Permissions are unchanged (vibrate only).
  • Rulings:
    1. Who decides: both. Only a store the mall marks as walk-through (Unit.PassThrough, L29) and that is open (StoreStatus.IsRoutable, L83) can be cut through, and only when the shopper allows "Shortcuts through stores" in Me. The setting is off by default and saved on the phone (L88). Not taken: the demo's rule that any open store with two or more doors qualifies.
    2. Only way in: a marked store that is the only way to a place is a passage, not a shortcut. Routes use it whatever the setting, and the step says "Walk through store". The setting controls optional shortcuts only.
    3. Passages are worked out at publish: the publish engine finds the places that can't be reached without a marked store, and marks those stores as passages for them. The admin sets nothing extra. Not taken: a second store flag, Shortcut or Passage, set by the mall.
    4. Wheelchair: never a shortcut, as the demo. A passage is used, or the place could not be reached at all. L88's 1.2 m door width still applies to its doors.
    5. Evacuation: never a shortcut, as the demo. A store that is the only way out is used. This goes into the L27 safety and fire-code review.
    6. Store weight: the walk across a store counts 1.25× its length, a setting each mall can tune, like the L86/L91 connector costs. The steps show the real metres.
    7. Wall check: publish is refused, with the place shown, if any generated or admin-corrected walkway crosses a wall. This adds to PRD §6.3's checks (L32).
    8. As the demo: the step text and icon, Find another way around the whole store with its tip, and the route worked out again when the setting changes.
  • Opening hours stay P2 (L33, PRD §6.16): no shortcut through a store that closes before the shopper would get out.
  • Expectations: no new table or column. Only the Unit.PassThrough comment changes, and the weight is a setting. The publish engine (P0-03) tags each walk-through edge with its unit and marks passages; routing (P0-06) gates them.
  • Risks: an unmarked store that is really the only way in leaves a place unreachable. PRD §6.3's "every store reachable" check stops that publish. A marked store whose door is locked on the day has no live status until P2 (L33); the shopper uses Find another way.

L932026-10-05Admin web page on Firebase Hosting (Hosting only); the backend VM and the database stay with D10a and D10b 0.38The product owner: "the admin webpage shall be deployed on firebase", a call made on their own authority ("i have all the privileges to make a call"). It takes effect now, with no pending sign-off.
  • Ruling: the admin web page (the Flutter Web build) is served by Firebase Hosting. Only Hosting is used, on the no-cost Spark plan: no Firebase Analytics, Auth, Crashlytics or database, so nothing about admins or shoppers goes to Firebase. Sign-in stays jsonwebtoken + bcryptjs.
  • Choices (interview):
    • Address: <project>.web.app now, the company's own domain (for example admin.<domain>) later.
    • Ownership: the Firebase project sits under a Dynamiq company Google account, with the product owner and one other named person as Owners.
    • Deploys, from Phase 1: each PR gets a Firebase preview link; going live happens after merge, only with a named release approval (the standard's Deploy gate).
    • Scope: the admin page only. The P2-01 web directory's hosting is decided when P2 starts.
  • Consequences:
    • Hosting rewrites can target only local files, Cloud Functions or Cloud Run, not an outside server, so the page calls the API at its own address. CORS_ORIGINS lists the Firebase address. Admin sign-in uses tokens saved on the device, not cookies, so the separate address needs no cookie changes.
    • Hosting's 60-second request limit applies only to rewrites; the admin SSE stream goes straight to the VM.
    • DESIGN CHOICE: the page's security headers (CSP, frame-ancestors, nosniff) are set in firebase.json, because helmet covers only the API. Alternative: no headers on the static page, which leaves it frameable.
    • The standard's verbatim middleware order ends "static/uploads → SPA fallback". The admin page's fallback is now Hosting's rewrite: EXCEPTIONS E-05, signed by the product owner.
    • D10b (Raphael) narrows to the backend VM and the database. D10a is unchanged.
  • OPEN QUESTIONS: which API the preview links call (a staging API?) and how CORS admits preview addresses; the company domain for the later switch; the second named Owner.
  • Expectations: no schema change. A firebase.json and the deploy workflow arrive with the Phase 1 rails. firebase-tools is pinned in Blueprint Part 1.
  • Risks: the Spark plan disables a site that passes 10 GB of transfer in a month; an admin page is far below that, and Blaze is the fallback. A CSP written too tightly can break the Flutter Web build, so it is tested on a preview link first.

L982026-10-06Hosting: the whole backend on Firebase (SQL Connect/PostgreSQL in Singapore, one Cloud Function, Firebase Auth); supersedes D10a and D10b; the §1 deviations are recorded on the product owner's authority 0.42The product owner: "change of plan, we will now deploy the app on firebase including the database". Two interview rounds settled the shape. Four of the eight answers go against the recommendation; this row records them as the product owner's.
  • Rulings:
    1. Database: Firebase SQL Connect (formerly Data Connect), PostgreSQL on Cloud SQL. Recommended: Cloud SQL for SQL Server, which keeps §1, schema.sql and the T-SQL unchanged. Not taken: Firestore.
    2. Runtime: Cloud Functions for Firebase, 2nd gen, Express inside one HTTPS function (onRequest(app)). Recommended: a Cloud Run container, which keeps Node 26 and the single-process design intact.
    3. Sign-in: Firebase Auth for admins and shoppers. Recommended: keep jsonwebtoken + bcryptjs, which needs no deviation and keeps identity data out of Firebase.
    4. Sign-off: the product owner's ruling, no further sign-off. Recommended: the owner rules and Raphael signs the §1 parts, as §1 requires.
    5. Region: Singapore, asia-southeast1, as recommended. Firebase Auth runs only in US data centres whatever the region.
    6. Admin live status: SSE straight to the function's own URL, not through Hosting (whose rewrites cut at 60 s), as recommended.
    7. Instances: maxInstances = 1 for the pilot, as recommended, so the single-process design (permission cache, SSE hub, rate limiter, scheduler) stays true. The cap is lifted when the shared stores the Blueprint names are built.
    8. Schema: ported to PostgreSQL now, in Phase 0, as recommended. db/schema.sql is PostgreSQL; the T-SQL file is kept as db/schema.mssql.sql.
  • What stays: Express; raw parameterized SQL (now through pg) and the no-ORM rule; decimal.js with DECIMAL(18,4) (PostgreSQL's alias of NUMERIC); every invariant, with 12 reworded (TIMESTAMPTZ); D6 packages as SQLite files, built in the function and written to Cloud Storage; the admin page on Firebase Hosting (L93, E-05); the §3.4 route × CRUD permission tables, keyed to the Firebase UID through AppUser.FirebaseUid; invariant 4 (positions never reach the server).
  • What changes: D10a's VM becomes one function; D10b closes (Google Cloud, Singapore; Raphael's call ends); SQL Server → PostgreSQL; mssql → pg; jsonwebtoken + bcryptjs → Firebase Auth ID tokens verified with firebase-admin, so AppUser.PasswordHash, the lockout and single-session columns, RefreshToken and DC10 are retired (Firebase handles refresh and brute-force protection); the VM file store → Cloud Storage for Firebase (StoredFile keeps the SHA-256; StoragePath becomes the object path); the in-process scheduler → scheduled functions; SSE served from the function URL; Node 26 → 22 (the Functions runtime); MSSQL temporal teardown → none (AuditLog is the history); IDENTITY → GENERATED ALWAYS AS IDENTITY; filtered unique indexes → partial indexes; ISJSON → JSONB; UNIQUEIDENTIFIER → UUID.
  • The standard: §1 denies PostgreSQL by name, mandates mssql, jsonwebtoken and bcryptjs, and says a deviation needs the lead's written sign-off in EXCEPTIONS.md before the repo exists (Allan Young; Raphael Mendoza where Allan works on the project). CLAUDE.md: "the engineering standards takes precedence". The product owner chose not to seek the sign-off. E-06 (PostgreSQL), E-07 (pg), E-08 (Firebase Auth) and E-09 (firebase-admin, firebase-functions) are approved by the product owner, and each says that §1 names the lead. The P0 gate item "every §1 deviation is either reversed or has written lead sign-off" is open until Allan or Raphael countersigns. This is stated in the brief for them.
  • Data location: database, functions and files in Singapore; Firebase Auth in the United States. OQ5 is reopened and widened from PII encryption to data location: an OPEN QUESTION for the DPO under RA 10173. No residency rule is asserted here. PRD §2's "shopper position never leaves the phone" is unaffected.
  • DESIGN CHOICES: maxInstances = 1 (alternative: shared rate-limit store, permission cache and job lock now); SSE at the function URL (alternative: admins poll like shoppers); roles and permissions stay in AppUser/UserRole/RolePermission with Firebase holding only credentials (alternative: custom claims in the ID token, which would move the §3.4 model out of the database); the schema ported by hand (alternative: SQL Connect's own schema language, whose support for composite FKs, triggers and CHECK sets is unverified).
  • OPEN QUESTIONS: the DPO's view on data location; the lead's countersignature; Cloud SQL's PostgreSQL price in Singapore (not verified); better-sqlite3's native build inside the Functions runtime and its in-memory /tmp (RECALLED: works; OQ9); the pg connection path (the Cloud SQL connector against a public IP with authorised networks); the shopper sign-in providers (D1b, still P0).
  • Expectations: Blaze billing from day one (SQL Connect and Cloud SQL need it; Cloud SQL "starts as low as $9.37 / month"; 250,000 SQL Connect operations a month free, then $0.90 per million). /api is called at the function URL, as L93 already said. Cold starts with one instance; minInstances = 1 costs money and is left as a setting.
  • Risks: the plan now runs against §1 in three places with no lead signature; if Allan or Raphael objects, the database choice is the costliest to unwind (the schema port is reversible: the T-SQL file is kept). A function that scales past 1 breaks the in-memory limiter and cache, so the cap is a release-blocking setting. Firebase Auth outages are outside Gabay's control.

    • Language: English or everyday Tagalog for all everyday wording and the voice, from a phrase table (lib/core/tagalog.dart) with a test that every phrase has a translation. Shop and level names and the Technical wording stay as they are.
    • Turn-by-turn directions, landmark first (lib/venue/directions.dart): "Turn left at Zen Gifts · in 20 m · past the ATM"; a big card with an arrow in the route sheet, the full list under it. A bend of 30° or more is a turn; a slight bend (30–50°) only at a fork; a corridor that merely curves gets no word. Metres count down as you walk.
    • Voice guidance (lib/state/voice.dart, narration.dart), off by default: the phone's text-to-speech in English (Philippines) or Filipino, worded like a person; a turn within 25 m of the one before is said with it; metres only where there is nothing to name; "Coming up: …" and "Turn left now"; off a ride, the new level and what should be around. A one-deep queue: sentences are never cut off, only alerts, the emergency, arriving and answers interrupt, and a stale cue is dropped rather than said late. A mute button on the rail. Checkpoints every 25–40 m on a walk over 40 m, "Getting close now", "Almost there", "This should be it", each once. Stairs and escalators say each landing, an express escalator only its stops, a lift nothing between.
    • Never stated as fact: because the dot is simulated, the voice says what you should see.     • Felt and heard: vibrate before each turn (one short buzz left, two right, one long before a ride, three for an alert); a wrong-way sound (assets/sounds/faaah.mp3, off by default) when the shopper answers "No, I'm somewhere else", then "Looks like you've drifted off the way.
